# Open questions ledger

**This is an INTAKE channel, not a holding pen** (user directive 2026-05-28). Any agent appends a question here when it surfaces. It is **not** where open work accumulates indefinitely — actionable questions are *migrated into the plan* (`scaffolding/priorities.md`, the project's ongoing fan-out-ranked work plan); resolved/stale/duplicate ones are *closed* to the index below; genuinely-blocked ones are *kept, compacted, with their trigger condition*.

**Maintenance protocol (meta-phase owns this):**
- Between meta-phases the ledger is **append-only** (any agent appends; `integrator-per-report` promotes per-report open questions).
- At **every meta-phase**, the meta-phase runs the unification pass (see `.claude/agents/meta-phase.md` §OQ-ledger unification): triage each `Open` entry against the firm artifact → **close** (resolved/stale/duplicate), **migrate** (actionable → `priorities.md` backlog, ranked by fan-out impact), or **keep** (deferred/contingent, one-liner + trigger). Methodology/policy questions only the human can resolve are flagged `routes to meta-phase / human` and surfaced as an `ask`.
- `cycle-planner` reads this ledger each primary cycle for fresh-intake plan candidates; it does not edit it.

### assemble-diagonal / diagonal-preconditioning family
*(opened_at: cycle-019, opened_by: harvester — from the firm L1 `assemble-diagonal` harvest)*
- `assemblediagonal-is-not-apply-linop-variant` — **RESOLVED by this harvest** (resolution-anchor: `book/src/L1/assemble-diagonal.md`, firm cycle-019). The deliberate exclusion-marker is now answered: `assemble_diagonal` lands as its own firm L1 entry with the operator/data divide stated explicitly (`apply_linop :: (A, x) -> y` is operator-and-vector-to-vector; `assemble_diagonal :: (A) -> Tensor[N]` is operator-to-data, consuming no vector — the result is a property of `A` alone). - `assemble-diagonal-mutation-rotation` — the L1>L0 lowering theme for `assemble_diagonal` is not yet authored (abstractor). Must narrate forward from L1 `d = assemble_diagonal(A)` to the L0 `A.AssembleDiagonal(diag)` output-arg mutation, absorbing: (i) destination sizing / `diag = 0.0` zero-init; (ii) the representation split (Hypre CSR exact read vs libCEED element-local accumulation vs `ParOperator` abs-prolongation-transpose `|P|ᵀ dₗ`); (iii) the Dirichlet `DiagonalPolicy` BC post-step; (iv) the matrix-free high-order-Nedelec approximation as a load-bearing assembly-order property. **Backlog migration** — pair with the existing `diagonal-extraction-l1` plan item. *Trigger:* an abstractor L1>L0 dispatch on `assemble_diagonal` (now-firm L1 anchor ready).
- `assemble-diagonal-reciprocal-elementwise-product-l1-primitives` — the diagonal-preconditioner apply completes via `mfem::Vector::Reciprocal` (`dinv.Reciprocal()`) + an element-wise product (`Apply(dinv, x, y)`, `palace/linalg/jacobi.cpp:104`); both referenced as **plain text** in the `assemble-diagonal` entry (forthcoming `reciprocal` / `elementwise_product` L1 primitives, not yet authored — singletons, not stub-materialized this pass). Harvesting them would let the Jacobi/Chebyshev preconditioner-apply be expressed as a clean L1 composition `y = elementwise_product(reciprocal(assemble_diagonal(A)), x)`. **Backlog migration** — candidate plan items. *Trigger:* a harvester dispatch on `reciprocal` / `elementwise_product` (or a consumer needing the clean composition).
- `assemble-diagonal-mfem-real-path-upstream` — the real-operator `AssembleDiagonal` resolves into vendored MFEM via `using Operator = mfem::Operator` (`palace/linalg/operator.hpp:21`); Palace overrides it only on its own subclasses (`HypreCSRMatrix`, `ParOperator`, libCEED `Operator`). Per CLAUDE.md "Many symbols resolve into upstream libraries", the L1 entry cites Palace call sites + overrides, not the MFEM base virtual's internals. The exact semantics of `mfem::Operator::AssembleDiagonal` for any Palace real-operator that does NOT override it (and inherits the MFEM default) is out-of-scope evidence — does NOT block firm status (all surfaced smoother consumers call into overriding subclasses). *Trigger:* a future cross-layer pass should confirm no real-operator consumer relies on the MFEM default behaviour.

### divfree Mult doc-comment fidelity (opened_at: cycle-019, opened_by: cross-layer-cross-cutter)
- `divfree-mult-doc-irrotational-vs-divfree-stale` — **RESOLVED-by-this-report; closure-ready, meta-phase to close + migrate to the Closed index.** Carry-forward OQ (originated L1 entry cycle-013, re-surfaced cycle-016, tracked only via the plan `priorities.md:27` + `cycle-019-resume-notes.md:88`, NOT previously in this ledger). A cross-layer-cross-cutter dispatch (`reports/2026-05-29T023000Z-cross-layer-cross-cutter-divfree-doc/`) adjudicated it **(a) resolve** with firsthand L0 verification. **Disposition: answered — Palace-internal stale per-method doc-comment.** `palace/linalg/divfree.hpp:64-66` ("the irrotational portion … satisfying ∇ × y = 0") is **inverted**: in the Helmholtz/Hodge decomposition `y = y_divfree + Grad·ψ` the irrotational (`Grad·ψ`) component is the part the projector *removes*; the comment names the removed part where it should name the divergence-free remainder. The authoritative L0 site is the **class** doc `palace/linalg/divfree.hpp:28-31` ("projection onto a divergence-free space satisfying `Gᵀ M x = 0`"), realised by the implementation `palace/linalg/divfree.cpp:155-190`; a third independent L0 witness is the inline impl comment `palace/linalg/divfree.cpp:176` ("Compute the irrotational portion of y and subtract."). No artifact defect — the firm entries already pin the correct divergence-free semantics (`book/src/L1/divfree-projector.md:145-150`, `book/src/L1-L0/divfree-projector-mutation-rotation.md:460-468`, the latter sharpened this cycle to record the resolution self-contained). **No re-anchor, no lifter follow-up; upstream comment-fix is out of project scope.** *Trigger:* meta-phase unify-pass — move to the Closed index and flip the `priorities.md:27` plan item to resolved.

### combinator-miner parametric-family mode — first live exercise (opened_at: cycle-019, opened_by: combinator-miner)
*(from `reports/2026-05-29T023000Z-combinator-miner-parametric-family/` — the FIRST live exercise of the cycle-018-enacted parametric/variadic-family detection mode. PROPOSAL / mode-validation report; emitted NO `book/` mutation — the `inner_product` L2 rough-in row already stands from cycle-018. Two of the items below are **methodology-agenda intake for the next meta-phase**; the rest are harvester-input characterization for the in-flight `inner_product` harvester.)*

- `inner-product-fold-family-mode-characterization` — **harvester-input (feeds the in-flight cycle-019 `inner_product` L2 harvester, integration #7 next).** The parametric-family mode independently surfaced the `inner_product` reduce-to-scalar fold as ONE family across three structured parameter axes — **conjugation convention** (`dot` Hermitian `conj(x[i])·y[i]` vs `tdot`/`TransposeDot` bilinear `x[i]·y[i]`), **element-type** (real `Vector` fold lifted componentwise to complex `ComplexVector` over (Re,Im), `vector.cpp:674-685`), **weight presence** (`M=I` plain vs pre-applied `M`) — plus a fourth **diagonal-degeneration** value (`y=x` → norm-squared, the `nrm2`/`matrix-weighted-norm` *consumer*, NOT a fold member). The **fold-law membership test** (the evidence the cohort is one family, not a cluster): split-additivity / concatenation-homomorphism `inner_product (x₁++x₂)(y₁++y₂) = inner_product x₁ y₁ + inner_product x₂ y₂` — a monoid homomorphism `(length-concat,++) → (Scalar,+)`, mirroring the firm `linear_combination` concatenation-homomorphism (`book/src/L2/linear_combination.md:117-124`). Over-unification guards (REQUIRED, all three hold): do NOT merge with `linear_combination` (reduce-to-`Tensor[N]`, different result-type/combining-step/fold-axis — boundary already drawn `L2/index.md:25-26`); do NOT subsume `nrm2`/`matrix-weighted-norm` (`√ ∘ inner_product` consumers); do NOT subsume the smoother cohort (operator-action, not a fold — see the mode-gap item below). **Post-repair**, the M-weighted member's L0 form is `yᴴ A x` (Palace's `Dot(comm,x,A,y)` builds `Ax=A·x` then `Dot(comm,Ax,y)`, and `Dot` conjugates its SECOND operand `y`; `operator.cpp:621-628`, `vector.cpp:263-267`, `vector.hpp:246`), matching the standing stub `book/src/L2/inner_product.md:7` — the harvester inherits the corrected math directly. Conjugation-pinning is the hard must-resolve-before-firm (already tracked, see next item). *Trigger:* the in-flight `inner_product` L2 harvester folds this characterization into its firm entry (no separate dispatch needed — it is the next integration this cycle).
- `inner-product-tdot-uncalled-evidentiary-weight` — `tdot`/`ComplexVector::TransposeDot` (`vector.hpp:112` decl + `vector.cpp:269-274` def) has **zero call sites** in the Palace tree (independently confirmed by the critic's `search_text TransposeDot` over `palace/**` → only the decl+def lines). It is a real, defined kernel and a legitimate conjugation-axis value (the unconjugated bilinear form), but its evidentiary weight is **type-API-surface only, not behavioral**. Combinator-miner recommends keeping it as a family member with a "no Palace call site" caveat (cf. `bilinear-form`'s narrow-coverage note) rather than demoting it to a documented-but-not-instantiated axis value. **Question for the harvester:** cite `tdot` as firm (matching `book/src/L1/dot.md` which treats it as a co-defined operator) or rough-in? *Trigger:* the `inner_product` harvester decides the citation tier (same in-flight dispatch).
- `inner-product-weighted-split-additivity-whole-vector-only` — the split-additivity homomorphism holds elementwise for the plain/conjugate members, but for `inner_product_M` the *per-block* split requires `M` block-diagonal w.r.t. the split; the *whole-vector* form always holds (one fold over the M-applied operand). The harvester should state the law at whole-vector granularity to avoid over-claiming a per-block tiling law for the weighted member. *Trigger:* the `inner_product` harvester firm-up (same in-flight dispatch).
- **Conjugation-pinning** — already tracked by the active plan OQ `inner-product-harvester-formalization-and-conjugation-pinning` (§Open — migrated to the plan, plan **Now #1/#2**). This report RAISES it to a hard must-resolve-before-firm and supplies the resolved L0 reading (`yᴴ A x` for the weighted member; the L1 `dot.md:34,43` deliberate arg-1-conjugate re-order is an intentional L1 mutation-rotation, NOT a Palace-internal contradiction — the report self-corrected to that framing, critic-affirmed). No new OQ slug; the existing plan item owns it. *Trigger:* already plan-active.

### Routes to meta-phase — combinator-miner parametric-family mode-gap (opened_at: cycle-019, opened_by: combinator-miner)
*(methodology-agenda intake for the NEXT meta-phase to adjudicate; mirror into `priorities.md` §Next-meta-phase methodology agenda. This is the load-bearing mode-validation finding from the first live exercise — feeds friction-ledger `combinator-miner-arity-blind-parametric-family-detection` resolution.)*
- `combinator-miner-nonfold-parametric-family-no-positive-channel` (Qualification B — the load-bearing finding) — **the parametric/variadic-family mode performed to spec on the fold case (validated: it independently surfaced the `inner_product` cohort as one candidate, catching a fold-family that arity-blind same-shape counting would see only as 3 too-thin leaves — `tdot` is 0 call sites), but it found a genuine spec gap.** The broad scan turned up a strong parametric cohort the mode's spec does NOT cover: the **smoother / constructed-operator-action family** — `JacobiSmoother` (`palace/linalg/jacobi.hpp:19`), `ChebyshevSmoother` + `ChebyshevSmoother1stKind` (`palace/linalg/chebyshev.hpp:23,86`), `DistRelaxationSmoother` (`palace/linalg/distrelaxation.hpp:30`), all `: public Solver<OperType>`, all templated `<Operator>`/`<ComplexOperator>` (element-type axis), all implementing one `Mult(x,y)` fixed-cost relaxation action parameterized by a relaxation kernel (Jacobi diagonal-scale / Chebyshev polynomial / hybrid DistRelaxation). This is unmistakably a **parametric family along element-type + relaxation-kernel** — BUT it is `Tensor[N] → Tensor[N]` **operator-action-shaped, NOT a fold** (no `-> Scalar`/`-> Tensor[N]` reduction, no combining-step identity, no concatenation-homomorphism). The mode's "if you cannot state a unifying fold/parametric law it is NOT a parametric family" guard *correctly excludes* it from a fold-candidate proposal — but the spec gives **no positive channel** for "this IS a structured parametric family, just not a fold one." Non-blocking here (no row lost; the cohort is already partly captured by `book/src/L1/chebyshev-smoother.md` + the `nested-constructed-operator-gate` concept), but a future non-fold parametric cohort with no existing capture could fall through the crack. **Recommendation for the meta-phase:** extend the parametric-family mode (or add a sibling note) to recognize **constructed-operator-action families** (parametric over kernel + element-type, unified by a shared `Solver<OperType>::Mult` action contract `y' = relax(op, x, y, initial_guess)` rather than a fold-law) as a distinct, reportable family class. Without this the mode is fold-complete but parametric-family-incomplete. *Routes to:* next meta-phase methodology adjudication.
- `variant-absorption-vs-instance-counting-policy` confirmation — the existing meta-phase-agenda OQ (§Routes to meta-phase, line ~95) asked whether the cycle-018 parametric-family mode addresses the variant-absorption-vs-instance-counting question. **This first live exercise CONFIRMS it does, with a refinement worth recording:** the mode is *concordant and complementary* with same-shape instance-counting (both point at the same L2 `inner_product` fold — cycle-018's same-shape pass already cleared the ≥3-instance bar and landed the rough-in), and the parametric-family mode's **distinctive deliverable is the fold-law membership-test + axis taxonomy, even when same-shape already surfaced the row** — i.e. it earns its keep as a *characterizer*, not only as a *surfacer*. The meta-phase can use this to close `variant-absorption-vs-instance-counting-policy` (the existing slug; close authority is meta-phase per §Write-authority partition — I only append this confirmation as intake). *Routes to:* next meta-phase (close `variant-absorption-vs-instance-counting-policy` with this confirmation; record the characterizer-deliverable refinement in the friction-ledger `combinator-miner-arity-blind-parametric-family-detection` resolution).

The contingent count-dependency (4↔3) is therefore RESOLVED at 4 — no follow-up −1 adjustment to the `L4/index.md` tally. Per the batch-24 meta-phase 2nd-gate sharpening (decision (e)), gate (b) is dischargeable in write-scope by a `find-tests-for-region`/`lowering-verifier` pass CITING the existing `test-domainpostoperator.cpp` postprocess coverage (the whole-domain `GetElectricFieldEnergy` SI-energy assertion `:83,87-91` witnesses the energy-FORM constituent, but NOT the per-domain restriction + participation assembly). It is DISTINCT from `Measurement::DomainData` (the OUTPUT row, OQ `record-DomainData-needs-definition-home`). *Trigger:* if a per-domain output-surface chapter (e.g. a `postoperatorcsv` domain-table writer or a lifecycle postprocess-output stage) becomes a 2nd consumer naming `DomainOpMap`, promote to a `concepts/DomainOpMap.md` record-definition page (the ≥2-consumer bar, the `record` Kind under `concepts/index.md`).
- `domain-field-energy-participation-guard-inconsistency` (opened c079 D3; possible `problems/` drive-by intake) — Palace's `MeasureDomainFieldEnergy` guards the participation division INCONSISTENTLY: the electric pass guards on the numerator (`std::abs(energy_i) > 0.0`, `postoperator.cpp:1039`), the magnetic pass guards on the denominator (`std::abs(energy) > 0.0`, `:1064`). The two are not behavior-equivalent in the edge case `energyᵢ > 0, e_total = 0` (electric divides by zero; magnetic returns 0). The L4 verb adopts the uniform denominator guard (the safe one). ## USER DIRECTIVE 2026-06-03 (cycle-082→083 window; for the batch-26 meta-phase to ENACT)

- **`feature-column-promotion-break-the-seed-deadlock` (USER DIRECTIVE — HIGH; for the batch-26 meta-phase to enact as a methodology amendment).** The user observed all 13 FEATURE-SURFACE SPINE columns (36/36 files) are stuck at `status: seed` and asked whether anything actively drives them. Diagnosis: the per-column promotion rule each column's prose invented — "promote past `seed` only once **ALL** constituents are firm" — combined with the output-product↔driver reciprocal cross-linking creates a **mutual-blocking deadlock**: `eigenmode.L4` (driver) stays seed because it reduces into `eigenfrequency-qfactor` (output-product, seed), and `eigenfrequency-qfactor` stays seed because its constituent `eigenmode.L4` driver column is seed — each names the other as the non-firm blocker, so no column can EVER promote (even after the verb between them, `eigenfreq_qfactor_reduce`, was firmed c082). **THE DIRECTIVE — break the deadlock rule:** a column promotes off `seed` when its **OWN composition + its directly-owned constituents** are firm; **cross-linked SIBLING columns are treated as references, NOT blocking constituents.** A driver column promotes on its firm solve/assemble combinators + its own readout, independent of the output-product column it cross-links (and vice-versa). **Enactment (batch-26 meta-phase, ALL go):** (1) amend the column-promotion convention in CLAUDE.md §Extraction-goal (FEATURE-SURFACE SPINE) + the `layer-intro-author` role-spec §FEATURE-SURFACE; (2) queue a `layer-intro-author` re-evaluation of ALL 13 columns under the new rule (re-author each column's promotion-rule prose + flip the status token of every column whose own composition + directly-owned constituents are now firm) — likely the batch-27 lead; (3) fix the now-stale `eigenmode.L4:55` clause (calls `eigenfreq_qfactor_reduce` rough-in; firm as of c082). The `.claude/agents/` edit → SESSION RESTART after the meta-phase. Memory: `project_feature_column_promotion_rule`. *Trigger:* batch-26 meta-phase (fires after c084) — this is the highest-priority meta-phase enactment item.

- `feature-column-promotion-break-the-seed-deadlock` (USER DIRECTIVE 2026-06-03) — **ENACTED-CLOSED (GO).** The OWN-COMPOSITION column-promotion rule is codified into CLAUDE.md §Extraction-goal (FEATURE-SURFACE SPINE) + the `layer-intro-author` role-spec §FEATURE-SURFACE: a column promotes off `seed` when its OWN composition + directly-owned constituents are firm; cross-linked SIBLING columns are references, NOT blockers. This breaks the `eigenmode`↔`eigenfrequency-qfactor` mutual-blocking deadlock. The all-13-column re-evaluation under the new rule is **migrated to the plan as the batch-27 LEAD** (`scaffolding/priorities.md` §CYCLE-085 active head #1, `feature-column-seed-deadlock-break-re-evaluation`). The stale `eigenmode.L4:55` clause was confirmed already-clean (c083 hygiene + the eigenmode.L4 narration correctly calls `eigenfreq_qfactor_reduce` firm c082 and identifies the deadlock the new rule breaks). `.claude/agents/` + CLAUDE.md edits → SESSION RESTART before c085. Memory `project_feature_column_promotion_rule`.
- `eigenmode-driver-column-seed-promotion-blocks-eigenfrequency-qfactor-column` (opened c082) — **CLOSED-BY-RULE-CHANGE.** This OQ named the deadlock the directive above breaks (the `eigenmode` driver column blocking `eigenfrequency-qfactor` and vice-versa). Under the new OWN-COMPOSITION rule the inter-column blocker is dissolved: `eigenmode` promotes on its own firm `fe_assemble`×3 + firm `eigsolve`; `eigenfrequency-qfactor` promotes on its own firm `eigenfreq_qfactor_reduce` (c082). The actual `status:` flips are the batch-27 LEAD authoring work. Folds into `feature-column-promotion-break-the-seed-deadlock`.
- `output-product-reduce-verb-test-coverage-bounded-promotion-route` — **A1+A2 RESOLVED, NARROWED to the two-condition rule, A3/A4 foundation-gated (carried).** A2 `eigenfreq_qfactor_reduce` → firm c082; A1 `sparameter_reduce` → firm c083 — BOTH via the firm-on-positive-structure / syntactic-identity escape (the in-scope law-confidence route). Per the c083 narrowing: the escape applies to an output-product reduce-verb iff (i) ALL its folded L1 primitives are firm AND (ii) the assembly is bare scalar arithmetic with no axiom-needing law. A3 `gram_reduce` + A4 `domain_energy_reduce` do NOT yet meet (i) — their folded `matrix-weighted-norm`-family forms are still `rough-in (test-coverage-bounded)` (gated on the √-entry-point cascade). ## graded-stack-prose-status-inference-masks-untyped
opened_at: cycle-115
opened_by: layer-intro-author

The graded-stack linter's `derive_rank` falls back to the prose `## Status` line (`read_status_line`, anchored on the leading `` `firm` `` token) when frontmatter lacks `rank:`/`status:`. Consequence: a chapter with a firm `## Status` section but ZERO declared frontmatter `rank:`/`edges:` reports as **typed (rank 3)** and is **invisible to the `untyped` warning**, even though it carries zero declared graph edges. This is why the four cycle-115 D2 targets (`L1/fe_collection`, `L1-L0/{dot,nrm2,scal}-mutation-rotation`) did NOT show as untyped despite `grep -L "^edges:"` flagging them as edge-less. Two readings: (a) intended (prose status IS the rank signal during the P1 rollout); (b) a blind spot (a chapter can have zero declared edges yet never surface for typing). **(cycle-117 D5 — should the GSLIB point-interpolation facility get its own first-class L1>L0 obstruction theme?)** The GSLIB point/field-interpolation facility (`palace/fem/interpolator.{hpp,cpp}`: `fem::InterpolateFunction` ×2, the `InterpolationOperator` probe-field class, `ComputeLineIntegral`) is recorded **in-chapter as a sibling note** in the firm [`interpolator`](../book/src/L1/interpolator.md) chapter, disposed `obstruction (opaque-library-ownership)` (every entry point routes through `mfem::FindPointsGSLIB`, `#if defined(MFEM_USE_GSLIB)`-guarded with an `MFEM_ABORT` fallback; promotion route NONE). It is recorded in-chapter rather than as a separate L1>L0 obstruction theme because it is a *facility-level* boundary adjacent to (and orthogonal to) the firm de-Rham operator, not a lowering of `interpolator` itself. If a **feature-surface consumer** (e.g. a field-probe / line-integral output product) surfaces that needs the GSLIB facility as a first-class L1>L0 obstruction theme, that theme is a later dedicated dispatch.

*Trigger:* a feature-surface consumer (field-probe / point-sample / line-integral output product) lands that needs the GSLIB facility as a first-class obstruction theme; dispatch an abstractor to author `book/src/L1-L0/gslib-field-interpolation-obstruction.md` (sub-kind `opaque-library-ownership`).

## correction-step-one-vs-two-operator-conjugated-form
opened_at: cycle-121
opened_by: integrator-per-report
The conjugated correction form `y + T·B'·Tᵀ·(x − A·y)` (de-Rham auxiliary `T=G`, multigrid coarse-grid `T=P`) can be modeled two ways: (a) a single `correction_step` whose `B` is *any* LinOp (the conjugated `T·B'·Tᵀ` is just one such `B`), with the conjugation/Galerkin structure a *note*; or (b) `correction_step` + a distinct `conjugated_correction_step` tied by a conjugation law `conjugated_correction_step A T B' = correction_step A (T·B'·Tᵀ)`. The argument for (b): the transferred-space `B'` + transfer `T` is the form the distributive smoother and multigrid actually carry (the conjugation is not pre-formed). Both stated for the explore-and-coalesce multi-formulation discipline. **Action (harvester):** decide one-vs-two during formalization. Source: `reports/2026-06-07T054924Z-combinator-miner-kernel-shared-substrate/CYCLE.md` §Open-questions bullet 2.

## composite-as-l2-linear-combination-deferred-abstractor-pick
opened_at: cycle-122
opened_by: integrator-per-report
The 3D Grad+Curl flux-error **composite** (`TimeDependentFluxErrorEstimator` / `BoundaryModeFluxErrorEstimator`, `palace/linalg/errorestimator.cpp:536`/`:566`) is an elementwise sum over the **squared** per-element indicator vectors — `axpy(1, curl_estimates, grad_estimates)` / `linear_combination` over `Tensor[E]` indicator vectors. The c122-D1 `flux_recovery_estimate` harvest deliberately did NOT author it as a separate L1 verb (one operator per invocation; the composite COMPOSES two `flux_recovery_estimate` results, and minting a thin entry over `axpy(1,·)` — already firm — would be the mine-and-strand / identity-in-named-terms smell). The composite's shape is recorded in-chapter as law 4 (channel-additivity-over-squared-indicators) without minting a new entry. **Action (future abstractor / combinator-miner — DEMAND-GATED):** if a 2nd indicator-combining site surfaces, pick up the composite as an L2-level `linear_combination`-over-indicators node (NOT a new L1 verb). For now it stays an in-chapter law. Source: `reports/2026-06-07T071941Z-harvester-flux-recovery-estimate/CYCLE.md` §Open-questions (`Composite-as-L2-linear_combination is a deferred abstractor pick`).
